Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I... well, I hope I'm not taking up time from someone who needs it more. I've just been feeling a little out of sorts lately. 

I think what finally made me call—and it's a little embarrassing to admit, I really don't want you to think I'm being dramatic—is that I keep having these mornings where... where I just think it might be easier if I didn't wake up. I don't have a plan to hurt myself, of course! I would never actually do anything like that, I promise. But just having the thought kind of scared me enough to finally ask for some help. 

I hope it's okay that I came in for that. Am I making sense?

Well, um... I haven't been weighing myself, really, because I don't want to be fussy about it. But I suppose my clothes might be fitting just a little bit looser lately. 

I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything! It's just that... well, food just doesn't seem to call to me very much anymore. I'll make myself a little piece of toast in the morning, or maybe heat up some soup, just to have something. But then evening rolls around and I realize I've barely eaten anything all day. 

I know I need to do better about keeping my strength up, especially at my age. I'm really trying to make sure I get enough nutrition. Is it normal to lose your appetite a bit when you're grieving? I just hope I'm not causing any trouble for my body by being so forgetful about meals.

Well, um... I suppose I have been a little slower than usual, yes. I really hate to admit it, because I've always prided myself on being active and helpful. It's just that... well, food just doesn't seem to call to me very much anymore. I'll make myself a little piece of toast in the morning, or maybe heat up some soup, just to have something. But then evening rolls around and I realize I've barely eaten anything all day. \n\nI know I need to do better about keeping my strength up, especially at my age. I'm really trying to make sure I get enough nutrition. Is it normal to lose your appetite a bit when you're grieving? I just hope I'm not causing any trouble for my body by being so forgetful about meals.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling slowed down or lethargic since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose I have been a little slower than usual, yes. I really hate to admit it, because I've always prided myself on being active and helpful. Well, um... yes, I suppose it has been most days lately. I feel so terrible even admitting that out loud, because I know I have so much to be grateful for, and I really don't want to sound unappreciative of my life. 

But... well, honestly, that's actually what scared me enough to finally come in and ask for help. I just keep having these mornings where I open my eyes and I just think... maybe it would be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. 

Oh, but please don't worry! I don't have any sort of plan to hurt myself or anything like that. I would never, ever do something like that, I promise you. I know better than that. It's just... a thought that slips in before I can stop it, and then I try very hard to brush it away. I hope you don't think awfully of me for having thoughts like that. It's just a normal part of being overwhelmed sometimes, isn't it?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... no, I haven't stopped doing them altogether. I really try my best to keep up with my commitments, you know? I'd hate to let anyone down or seem anti-social. 

I still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the community dance night. But... um, well, to be perfectly honest, lately I suppose I just sit there feeling a bit like... like I'm behind a pane of glass? I'm physically there, but I'm just not quite taking it all in. 

And even my little hobbies at home... tending to my garden, or cooking a little extra for the neighbors... they just don't seem to give me that little lift they used to. I feel just awful admitting that to you, because I know I really *should* be enjoying them, and I promise I'm making the effort to stay engaged. I'm not just giving up. I hope it's alright that I'm feeling a little disconnected like this right now? Is it normal to lose that spark for a little while?

Oh, um... well, I suppose maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to dwell on things, because I know it doesn't do any good, but... I do find myself thinking about those last few weeks with her.

Oh, no... no, I don't believe so. I certainly hope I haven't come across as fidgety to you! I always try very hard to just sit quietly and, well, not make a fuss.

I just really wouldn't want to bother anyone by being restless or pacing about. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness, no, not the whole day. I really try my best to shake it off once I'm out of bed, I promise. I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around dwelling on dark things all afternoon. 

It's mostly just... well, it's in the mornings. I feel just awful even admitting this out loud, because I know I have a lot to be thankful for, but... I keep having these mornings where I just lie there and think it might be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. 

Honestly, having those thoughts is what scared me enough to finally come in and ask for help. I just want to be absolutely clear with you, though—I don't have any sort of plan to hurt myself or anything like that! I would never, ever do that. I know better than that. It's just this fleeting, heavy feeling when I first open my eyes. I hope it's alright that I'm sharing that with you? It's just a bit frightening to feel that way, and I want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by telling you.

Oh, um... well, I haven't really been keeping track on a scale or anything like that. I certainly haven't been trying to lose weight, I promise you that. I know it's important to keep my strength up.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really do try to keep up with everything, I promise. I haven't just stopped doing things or shut myself away. I still make myself go to my book club, and I still show up for the dance night at the community center, because I know people expect me there and I don't want to let anyone down. 

It's just that... oh, I feel terrible saying this, but lately when I'm there, I just kind of sit there feeling like I'm behind a pane of glass. Like I'm watching everyone else enjoy themselves, but I just can't quite reach that feeling myself. Even my garden, which I've always loved, or baking for the neighbors... it just doesn't give me that little lift it used to. 

I'm still doing it all, though! I wouldn't want you to think I'm just giving up on the things I love. I'm really trying to stay active. Do you think it's normal to feel a bit disconnected like that when you're grieving? I just hope I'm not doing something wrong by not enjoying it the way I should.

I'm really trying to make sure I get enough nutrition. Is it normal to lose your appetite a bit when you're grieving? I try so hard to just push through it, because I don't want to be a bother to anyone or seem lazy. I really am trying to keep my energy up. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not just making excuses for being tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the day since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say I feel sad or empty most of the day. I mean, naturally I miss her terribly, but I really try my hardest to keep a positive outlook. I don't want to walk around being a dark cloud for everyone else, you know? \n\nI always try to put on a brave face and count my blessings. So... no, not empty, really. Just a little out of sorts, like I mentioned before. I hope that doesn't make me sound cold, or like I'm not grieving her properly. Is it okay that I'm not crying all the time? I just really want to handle this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up nearly every day since they started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I suppose it has been most days lately. I feel so terrible even admitting that out loud, because I know I have so much to be grateful for, and I really don't want to sound unappreciative of my life. \n\nBut... well, honestly, that's actually what scared me enough to finally come in and ask for help. I just keep having these mornings where I open my eyes and I just think... maybe it would be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. \n\nOh, but please don't worry! I don't have any sort of plan to hurt myself or anything like that. I would never, ever do something like that, I promise you. I know better than that. It's just... a thought that slips in before I can stop it, and then I try very hard to brush it away. I hope you don't think awfully of me for having thoughts like that. It's just a normal part of being overwhelmed sometimes, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy before your sister passed away that you have since stopped doing altogether?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I haven't stopped doing them altogether. I really try my best to keep up with my commitments, you know? I'd hate to let anyone down or seem anti-social. \n\nI still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the community dance night. But... um, well, to be perfectly honest, lately I suppose I just sit there feeling a bit like... like I'm behind a pane of glass? I'm physically there, but I'm just not quite taking it all in. \n\nAnd even my little hobbies at home... tending to my garden, or cooking a little extra for the neighbors... they just don't seem to give me that little lift they used to. I feel just awful admitting that to you, because I know I really *should* be enjoying them, and I promise I'm making the effort to stay engaged. I'm not just giving up. I hope it's alright that I'm feeling a little disconnected like this right now? Is it normal to lose that spark for a little while?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things that wouldn't have bothered you before your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to dwell on things, because I know it doesn't do any good, but... I do find myself thinking about those last few weeks with her. \n\nI keep going over it in my head, and I just... I keep telling myself I should have called her more often, or maybe listened a little better when we did talk. Is it normal to feel this way after losing someone? I just really wouldn't want to bother anyone by being restless or pacing about. Is it okay that I haven't been feeling that way?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these thoughts of not wanting to wake up for most of the day, or are they more fleeting?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, not the whole day. I really try my best to shake it off once I'm out of bed, I promise. Oh, well... I know we just touched on this a moment ago, and I really don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm complaining too much. I promise I'm still trying my best to stay engaged. 

But, yes, I suppose it is most of the day. Even when I'm home alone, I just don't have that... that spark for my usual routines. Like I mentioned, I still force myself to go to book club and the community center dances, because I really don't want to be a burden or have people worrying about me. But I just sit there feeling so detached, like I'm watching it all from behind a pane of glass. I really am trying to keep busy, though! I'm not just sitting around doing nothing all day, I promise. It's just that the joy isn't quite there right now. Is it okay that I'm still going through the motions, even if I don't feel it? I just want to make sure I'm handling this the right way.

I hope that doesn't sound awful of me to say. \n\nAnd even when I'm home... my garden, or cooking little meals for the neighbors... those things usually make me so happy, but they just don't give me that little lift they used to. I'm sure it's just because I'm distracted, right? I really am trying my best to keep up with everything and be present. Am I explaining that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I haven't been weighing myself, really, because I don't want to be fussy about it. But I suppose my clothes might be fitting just a little bit looser lately. \n\nI'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything! It's just that... well, food just doesn't seem to call to me very much anymore. I'll make myself a little piece of toast in the morning, or maybe heat up some soup, just to have something. But then evening rolls around and I realize I've barely eaten anything all day. \n\nI know I need to do better about keeping my strength up, especially at my age. I'm really trying to make sure I get enough nutrition. Is it normal to lose your appetite a bit when you're grieving? I just hope I'm not causing any trouble for my body by being so forgetful about meals.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling slowed down or lethargic since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose I have been a little slower than usual, yes. I really hate to admit it, because I've always prided myself on being active and helpful. But lately... well, even just running simple errands, or trying to get the papers together for the Cultural Center... it leaves me feeling so terribly worn out. \n\nI know it sounds silly, but it almost feels like my body is moving through thick mud sometimes. I try so hard to just push through it, because I don't want to be a bother to anyone or seem lazy. I really am trying to keep my energy up. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not just making excuses for being tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the day since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say I feel sad or empty most of the day. I mean, naturally I miss her terribly, but I really try my hardest to keep a positive outlook. I don't want to walk around being a dark cloud for everyone else, you know? \n\nI always try to put on a brave face and count my blessings. So... no, not empty, really. Just a little out of sorts, like I mentioned before. I hope that doesn't make me sound cold, or like I'm not grieving her properly. Is it okay that I'm not crying all the time? I just really want to handle this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up nearly every day since they started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I suppose it has been most days lately. I feel so terrible even admitting that out loud, because I know I have so much to be grateful for, and I really don't want to sound unappreciative of my life. \n\nBut... well, honestly, that's actually what scared me enough to finally come in and ask for help. I just keep having these mornings where I open my eyes and I just think... maybe it would be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. \n\nOh, but please don't worry! I don't have any sort of plan to hurt myself or anything like that. I would never, ever do something like that, I promise you. I know better than that. It's just... a thought that slips in before I can stop it, and then I try very hard to brush it away. I hope you don't think awfully of me for having thoughts like that. It's just a normal part of being overwhelmed sometimes, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy before your sister passed away that you have since stopped doing altogether?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I haven't stopped doing them altogether. I really try my best to keep up with my commitments, you know? I'd hate to let anyone down or seem anti-social. \n\nI still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the community dance night. But... um, well, to be perfectly honest, lately I suppose I just sit there feeling a bit like... like I'm behind a pane of glass? I'm physically there, but I'm just not quite taking it all in. \n\nAnd even my little hobbies at home... tending to my garden, or cooking a little extra for the neighbors... they just don't seem to give me that little lift they used to. I feel just awful admitting that to you, because I know I really *should* be enjoying them, and I promise I'm making the effort to stay engaged. I'm not just giving up. I hope it's alright that I'm feeling a little disconnected like this right now? Is it normal to lose that spark for a little while?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things that wouldn't have bothered you before your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to dwell on things, because I know it doesn't do any good, but... I do find myself thinking about those last few weeks with her. \n\nI keep going over it in my head, and I just... I keep telling myself I should have called her more often, or maybe listened a little better when we did talk. I just feel like I should have done something more useful for her, you know? \n\nI mean, part of me knows—logically, anyway—that I couldn't have changed what happened. I really do know that. But I still feel this awful weight about it. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself. It's just hard not to feel like I let her down somehow. Is it normal to feel this way after losing someone? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I don't believe so. I certainly hope I haven't come across as fidgety to you! I always try very hard to just sit quietly and, well, not make a fuss. \n\nI haven't really felt the need to pace around or anything like that. If anything, I suppose I've just been sitting a bit more still than usual lately. I hope that's an alright way to handle things? I just really wouldn't want to bother anyone by being restless or pacing about. I know some people get very agitated when they're going through a hard time, but I promise I've just been trying to stay calm. Is it okay that I haven't been feeling that way?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these thoughts of not wanting to wake up for most of the day, or are they more fleeting?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, not the whole day. I really try my best to shake it off once I'm out of bed, I promise. I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around dwelling on dark things all afternoon. \n\nIt's mostly just... well, it's in the mornings. I feel just awful even admitting this out loud, because I know I have a lot to be thankful for, but... I keep having these mornings where I just lie there and think it might be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. \n\nHonestly, having those thoughts is what scared me enough to finally come in and ask for help. I just want to be absolutely clear with you, though—I don't have any sort of plan to hurt myself or anything like that! I would never, ever do that. I know better than that. It's just this fleeting, heavy feeling when I first open my eyes. I hope it's alright that I'm sharing that with you? It's just a bit frightening to feel that way, and I want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by telling you.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I haven't really been keeping track on a scale or anything like that. I certainly haven't been trying to lose weight, I promise you that. I know it's important to keep my strength up. \n\nIt's just that... well, food just doesn't really call to me much anymore. I'll make a point to fix myself a little something, maybe some toast or a small bowl of soup, because I know I should eat. Well, um... I haven't been keeping a strict eye on the scale or anything. I don't want you to think I'm neglecting my health, I really do try to take care of myself.

But lately... well, even just running simple errands, or trying to get the papers together for the Cultural Center... it leaves me feeling so terribly worn out. \n\nI know it sounds silly, but it almost feels like my body is moving through thick mud sometimes. I try so hard to just push through it, because I don't want to be a bother to anyone or seem lazy. I really am trying to keep my energy up. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not just making excuses for being tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the day since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say I feel sad or empty most of the day. I mean, naturally I miss her terribly, but I really try my hardest to keep a positive outlook. I don't want to walk around being a dark cloud for everyone else, you know? \n\nI always try to put on a brave face and count my blessings. So... no, not empty, really. Just a little out of sorts, like I mentioned before. I hope that doesn't make me sound cold, or like I'm not grieving her properly. Is it okay that I'm not crying all the time? I just really want to handle this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up nearly every day since they started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I suppose it has been most days lately. I feel so terrible even admitting that out loud, because I know I have so much to be grateful for, and I really don't want to sound unappreciative of my life. \n\nBut... well, honestly, that's actually what scared me enough to finally come in and ask for help. I just keep having these mornings where I open my eyes and I just think... maybe it would be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. \n\nOh, but please don't worry! I don't have any sort of plan to hurt myself or anything like that. I would never, ever do something like that, I promise you. I know better than that. It's just... a thought that slips in before I can stop it, and then I try very hard to brush it away. I hope you don't think awfully of me for having thoughts like that. It's just a normal part of being overwhelmed sometimes, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy before your sister passed away that you have since stopped doing altogether?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I haven't stopped doing them altogether. I really try my best to keep up with my commitments, you know? I'd hate to let anyone down or seem anti-social. \n\nI still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the community dance night. But... um, well, to be perfectly honest, lately I suppose I just sit there feeling a bit like... like I'm behind a pane of glass? I'm physically there, but I'm just not quite taking it all in. \n\nAnd even my little hobbies at home... tending to my garden, or cooking a little extra for the neighbors... they just don't seem to give me that little lift they used to. I feel just awful admitting that to you, because I know I really *should* be enjoying them, and I promise I'm making the effort to stay engaged. I'm not just giving up. I hope it's alright that I'm feeling a little disconnected like this right now? Is it normal to lose that spark for a little while?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things that wouldn't have bothered you before your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to dwell on things, because I know it doesn't do any good, but... I do find myself thinking about those last few weeks with her. \n\nI keep going over it in my head, and I just... I keep telling myself I should have called her more often, or maybe listened a little better when we did talk. I just feel like I should have done something more useful for her, you know? \n\nI mean, part of me knows—logically, anyway—that I couldn't have changed what happened. I really do know that. But I still feel this awful weight about it. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself. It's just hard not to feel like I let her down somehow. Is it normal to feel this way after losing someone? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I don't believe so. I certainly hope I haven't come across as fidgety to you! I always try very hard to just sit quietly and, well, not make a fuss. \n\nI haven't really felt the need to pace around or anything like that. If anything, I suppose I've just been sitting a bit more still than usual lately. I hope that's an alright way to handle things? I just really wouldn't want to bother anyone by being restless or pacing about. I know some people get very agitated when they're going through a hard time, but I promise I've just been trying to stay calm. Is it okay that I haven't been feeling that way?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these thoughts of not wanting to wake up for most of the day, or are they more fleeting?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, not the whole day. I really try my best to shake it off once I'm out of bed, I promise. I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around dwelling on dark things all afternoon. \n\nIt's mostly just... well, it's in the mornings. I feel just awful even admitting this out loud, because I know I have a lot to be thankful for, but... I keep having these mornings where I just lie there and think it might be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. \n\nHonestly, having those thoughts is what scared me enough to finally come in and ask for help. I just want to be absolutely clear with you, though—I don't have any sort of plan to hurt myself or anything like that! I would never, ever do that. I know better than that. It's just this fleeting, heavy feeling when I first open my eyes. I hope it's alright that I'm sharing that with you? It's just a bit frightening to feel that way, and I want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by telling you.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I haven't really been keeping track on a scale or anything like that. I certainly haven't been trying to lose weight, I promise you that. I know it's important to keep my strength up. \n\nIt's just that... well, food just doesn't really call to me much anymore. I'll make a point to fix myself a little something, maybe some toast or a small bowl of soup, because I know I should eat. But then I'll look at the clock in the evening and realize I've barely eaten anything all day. \n\nI really do try to eat, though! I don't want to be a bother or end up making myself sick. I just don't have much of an appetite lately. I hope you don't think I'm neglecting myself on purpose. Do you think it's okay if I just eat small things for now, until my appetite comes back?\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two months of these feelings since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, it hasn't been that long. I just wanted to be sure I wasn't wasting your time. But yes, it's just been this past month. I really hope I can get back to my normal self soon.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the things you used to enjoy have lost their appeal to you most of the time since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Oh, goodness, no... I don't think I'd use the word depressed. I mean, I really do try to keep my chin up, I promise. I know I mentioned feeling a bit disconnected earlier, but I wouldn't say I've been significantly down most of the day for weeks on end.

I just want to make sure I'm not wasting your time with something everyone goes through.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you normally enjoy since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I do still go to my activities. I really try not to let anyone down. I still make myself show up for things like my book club, or the local dance night. But, um... I don't know, lately I just sit there feeling like... like I'm behind a pane of glass? I'm there, but I'm not really participating the way I should be. I hope that doesn't sound awful of me to say. \n\nAnd even when I'm home... my garden, or cooking little meals for the neighbors... those things usually make me so happy, but they just don't give me that little lift they used to. I'm sure it's just because I'm distracted, right? I really am trying my best to keep up with everything and be present. Am I explaining that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I haven't been weighing myself, really, because I don't want to be fussy about it. But I suppose my clothes might be fitting just a little bit looser lately. \n\nI'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything! It's just that... well, food just doesn't seem to call to me very much anymore. I'll make myself a little piece of toast in the morning, or maybe heat up some soup, just to have something. But then evening rolls around and I realize I've barely eaten anything all day. \n\nI know I need to do better about keeping my strength up, especially at my age. I'm really trying to make sure I get enough nutrition. Is it normal to lose your appetite a bit when you're grieving? I just hope I'm not causing any trouble for my body by being so forgetful about meals.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling slowed down or lethargic since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose I have been a little slower than usual, yes. I really hate to admit it, because I've always prided myself on being active and helpful. But lately... well, even just running simple errands, or trying to get the papers together for the Cultural Center... it leaves me feeling so terribly worn out. \n\nI know it sounds silly, but it almost feels like my body is moving through thick mud sometimes. I try so hard to just push through it, because I don't want to be a bother to anyone or seem lazy. I really am trying to keep my energy up. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not just making excuses for being tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the day since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say I feel sad or empty most of the day. I mean, naturally I miss her terribly, but I really try my hardest to keep a positive outlook. I don't want to walk around being a dark cloud for everyone else, you know? \n\nI always try to put on a brave face and count my blessings. So... no, not empty, really. Just a little out of sorts, like I mentioned before. I hope that doesn't make me sound cold, or like I'm not grieving her properly. Is it okay that I'm not crying all the time? I just really want to handle this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up nearly every day since they started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I suppose it has been most days lately. I feel so terrible even admitting that out loud, because I know I have so much to be grateful for, and I really don't want to sound unappreciative of my life. \n\nBut... well, honestly, that's actually what scared me enough to finally come in and ask for help. I just keep having these mornings where I open my eyes and I just think... maybe it would be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. \n\nOh, but please don't worry! I don't have any sort of plan to hurt myself or anything like that. I would never, ever do something like that, I promise you. I know better than that. It's just... a thought that slips in before I can stop it, and then I try very hard to brush it away. I hope you don't think awfully of me for having thoughts like that. It's just a normal part of being overwhelmed sometimes, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy before your sister passed away that you have since stopped doing altogether?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I haven't stopped doing them altogether. I really try my best to keep up with my commitments, you know? I'd hate to let anyone down or seem anti-social. \n\nI still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the community dance night. But... um, well, to be perfectly honest, lately I suppose I just sit there feeling a bit like... like I'm behind a pane of glass? I'm physically there, but I'm just not quite taking it all in. \n\nAnd even my little hobbies at home... tending to my garden, or cooking a little extra for the neighbors... they just don't seem to give me that little lift they used to. I feel just awful admitting that to you, because I know I really *should* be enjoying them, and I promise I'm making the effort to stay engaged. I'm not just giving up. I hope it's alright that I'm feeling a little disconnected like this right now? Is it normal to lose that spark for a little while?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things that wouldn't have bothered you before your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to dwell on things, because I know it doesn't do any good, but... I do find myself thinking about those last few weeks with her. \n\nI keep going over it in my head, and I just... I keep telling myself I should have called her more often, or maybe listened a little better when we did talk. I just feel like I should have done something more useful for her, you know? \n\nI mean, part of me knows—logically, anyway—that I couldn't have changed what happened. I really do know that. But I still feel this awful weight about it. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself. It's just hard not to feel like I let her down somehow. Is it normal to feel this way after losing someone? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I don't believe so. I certainly hope I haven't come across as fidgety to you! I always try very hard to just sit quietly and, well, not make a fuss. \n\nI haven't really felt the need to pace around or anything like that. If anything, I suppose I've just been sitting a bit more still than usual lately. I hope that's an alright way to handle things? I just really wouldn't want to bother anyone by being restless or pacing about. I know some people get very agitated when they're going through a hard time, but I promise I've just been trying to stay calm. Is it okay that I haven't been feeling that way?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these thoughts of not wanting to wake up for most of the day, or are they more fleeting?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, not the whole day. I really try my best to shake it off once I'm out of bed, I promise. I keep having these mornings where I just lie there and think it might be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. \n\nHonestly, having those thoughts is what scared me enough to finally come in and ask for help. I just want to be absolutely clear with you, though—I don't have any sort of plan to hurt myself or anything like that! I would never, ever do that. I know better than that. It's just this fleeting, heavy feeling when I first open my eyes. I hope it's alright that I'm sharing that with you? It's just a bit frightening to feel that way, and I want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by telling you.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I haven't really been keeping track on a scale or anything like that. I just wanted to be sure I wasn't wasting your time. But yes, it's just been this past month. I know grief is normal, and I should probably just be handling it better, but... that's when it started. Is that... does that sound like a normal reaction to you? I just want to make sure I'm not wasting your time with something everyone goes through.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you normally enjoy since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I do still go to my activities. I really try not to let anyone down. I still make myself show up for things like my book club, or the local dance night. But, um... I don't know, lately I just sit there feeling like... like I'm behind a pane of glass? I'm there, but I'm not really participating the way I should be. I hope that doesn't sound awful of me to say. \n\nAnd even when I'm home... my garden, or cooking little meals for the neighbors... those things usually make me so happy, but they just don't give me that little lift they used to. I'm sure it's just because I'm distracted, right? I really am trying my best to keep up with everything and be present. Am I explaining that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I haven't been weighing myself, really, because I don't want to be fussy about it. But I suppose my clothes might be fitting just a little bit looser lately. \n\nI'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything! It's just that... well, food just doesn't seem to call to me very much anymore. I'll make myself a little piece of toast in the morning, or maybe heat up some soup, just to have something. But then evening rolls around and I realize I've barely eaten anything all day. \n\nI know I need to do better about keeping my strength up, especially at my age. I'm really trying to make sure I get enough nutrition. Is it normal to lose your appetite a bit when you're grieving? I just hope I'm not causing any trouble for my body by being so forgetful about meals.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling slowed down or lethargic since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose I have been a little slower than usual, yes. I really hate to admit it, because I've always prided myself on being active and helpful. But lately... well, even just running simple errands, or trying to get the papers together for the Cultural Center... it leaves me feeling so terribly worn out. \n\nI know it sounds silly, but it almost feels like my body is moving through thick mud sometimes. I try so hard to just push through it, because I don't want to be a bother to anyone or seem lazy. I really am trying to keep my energy up. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not just making excuses for being tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the day since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say I feel sad or empty most of the day. I mean, naturally I miss her terribly, but I really try my hardest to keep a positive outlook. I don't want to walk around being a dark cloud for everyone else, you know? \n\nI always try to put on a brave face and count my blessings. So... no, not empty, really. Just a little out of sorts, like I mentioned before. I hope that doesn't make me sound cold, or like I'm not grieving her properly. Is it okay that I'm not crying all the time? I just really want to handle this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up nearly every day since they started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I suppose it has been most days lately. I feel so terrible even admitting that out loud, because I know I have so much to be grateful for, and I really don't want to sound unappreciative of my life. \n\nBut... well, honestly, that's actually what scared me enough to finally come in and ask for help. I just keep having these mornings where I open my eyes and I just think... maybe it would be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. \n\nOh, but please don't worry! I don't have any sort of plan to hurt myself or anything like that. I would never, ever do something like that, I promise you. I know better than that. It's just... a thought that slips in before I can stop it, and then I try very hard to brush it away. I hope you don't think awfully of me for having thoughts like that. It's just a normal part of being overwhelmed sometimes, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy before your sister passed away that you have since stopped doing altogether?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I haven't stopped doing them altogether. I really try my best to keep up with my commitments, you know? I'd hate to let anyone down or seem anti-social. \n\nI still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the community dance night. But... um, well, to be perfectly honest, lately I suppose I just sit there feeling a bit like... like I'm behind a pane of glass? I'm physically there, but I'm just not quite taking it all in. \n\nAnd even my little hobbies at home... tending to my garden, or cooking a little extra for the neighbors... they just don't seem to give me that little lift they used to. I feel just awful admitting that to you, because I know I really *should* be enjoying them, and I promise I'm making the effort to stay engaged. I'm not just giving up. I hope it's alright that I'm feeling a little disconnected like this right now? Is it normal to lose that spark for a little while?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things that wouldn't have bothered you before your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to dwell on things, because I know it doesn't do any good, but... I do find myself thinking about those last few weeks with her. \n\nI keep going over it in my head, and I just... I keep telling myself I should have called her more often, or maybe listened a little better when we did talk. I just feel like I should have done something more useful for her, you know? \n\nI mean, part of me knows—logically, anyway—that I couldn't have changed what happened. I really do know that. But I still feel this awful weight about it. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself. It's just hard not to feel like I let her down somehow. Is it normal to feel this way after losing someone? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I don't believe so. I certainly hope I haven't come across as fidgety to you! I always try very hard to just sit quietly and, well, not make a fuss. \n\nI haven't really felt the need to pace around or anything like that. If anything, I suppose I've just been sitting a bit more still than usual lately. I hope that's an alright way to handle things? I just really wouldn't want to bother anyone by being restless or pacing about. I know some people get very agitated when they're going through a hard time, but I promise I've just been trying to stay calm. Is it okay that I haven't been feeling that way?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these thoughts of not wanting to wake up for most of the day, or are they more fleeting?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, not the whole day. I really try my best to shake it off once I'm out of bed, I promise. I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around dwelling on dark things all afternoon. \n\nIt's mostly just... well, it's in the mornings. I feel just awful even admitting this out loud, because I know I have a lot to be thankful for, but... I keep having these mornings where I just lie there and think it might be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. \n\nHonestly, having those thoughts is what scared me enough to finally come in and ask for help. I just want to be absolutely clear with you, though—I don't have any sort of plan to hurt myself or anything like that! I would never, ever do that. I know better than that. It's just this fleeting, heavy feeling when I first open my eyes. I hope it's alright that I'm sharing that with you? It's just a bit frightening to feel that way, and I want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by telling you.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I haven't really been keeping track on a scale or anything like that. I certainly haven't been trying to lose weight, I promise you that. I know it's important to keep my strength up. \n\nIt's just that... well, food just doesn't really call to me much anymore. I'll make a point to fix myself a little something, maybe some toast or a small bowl of soup, because I know I should eat. But then I'll look at the clock in the evening and realize I've barely eaten anything all day. \n\nI really do try to eat, though! I don't want to be a bother or end up making myself sick. I just don't have much of an appetite lately. I hope you don't think I'm neglecting myself on purpose. Do you think it's okay if I just eat small things for now, until my appetite comes back?\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two months of these feelings since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, it hasn't been that long. I wouldn't want you to think I've just been letting this drag on for months without trying to get a handle on it myself. I really do try to manage things. \n\nIt's only been about a month... ever since she passed away so unexpectedly. I tried my best to keep a brave face and just push through it, I really did. I didn't want to come running to a doctor right away if it was just normal grief, you know? I hope it was the right thing to wait a little while before coming in? I just wanted to be sure I wasn't wasting your time. But yes, it's just been this past month. I really hope I can get back to my normal self soon.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the things you used to enjoy have lost their appeal to you most of the time since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to keep up with everything, I promise. I haven't just stopped doing things or shut myself away. I still make myself go to my book club, and I still show up for the dance night at the community center, because I know people expect me there and I don't want to let anyone down. \n\nIt's just that... oh, I feel terrible saying this, but lately when I'm there, I just kind of sit there feeling like I'm behind a pane of glass. Like I'm watching everyone else enjoy themselves, but I just can't quite reach that feeling myself. Even my garden, which I've always loved, or baking for the neighbors... it just doesn't give me that little lift it used to. \n\nI'm still doing it all, though! I wouldn't want you to think I'm just giving up on the things I love. I'm really trying to stay active. Do you think it's normal to feel a bit disconnected like that when you're grieving? I just hope I'm not doing something wrong by not enjoying it the way I should.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have lost interest in things most of the day since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I know we just touched on this a moment ago, and I really don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm complaining too much. I promise I'm still trying my best to stay engaged. \n\nBut, yes, I suppose it is most of the day. Even when I'm home alone, I just don't have that... that spark for my usual routines. Like I mentioned, I still force myself to go to book club and the community center dances, because I really don't want to be a burden or have people worrying about me. But I just sit there feeling so detached, like I'm watching it all from behind a pane of glass. And even the quiet things at home, like tending to my garden or baking for the neighbors... they just don't give me that little lift they always used to. \n\nI really am trying to keep busy, though! I'm not just sitting around doing nothing all day, I promise. It's just that the joy isn't quite there right now. Is it okay that I'm still going through the motions, even if I don't feel it? I just want to make sure I'm handling this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant change in your weight since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I haven't been keeping a strict eye on the scale or anything. I don't want you to think I'm neglecting my health, I really do try to take care of myself. \n\nBut I suppose my clothes are fitting a little looser lately. It's just that... food just doesn't call to me much anymore. I'll fix myself a little piece of toast in the morning, or maybe heat up some soup, just to have something, you know? And then I'll realize by the evening that I've barely eaten anything else all day. \n\nI'm certainly not trying to lose weight on purpose, I promise. I know it's important to keep my strength up, especially at my age. I just sort of forget to eat, or I just don't have the stomach for a full meal right now. Is that... is that common when you're going through something like this? I hope I'm not causing any real harm by not eating as much as I usually do. I'll try to do better if you think I need to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling significantly depressed most of the day for at least two weeks since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no... I don't think I'd use the word depressed. I mean, I really do try to keep my chin up, I promise. I know I mentioned feeling a bit disconnected earlier, but I wouldn't say I've been significantly down most of the day for weeks on end. \n\nI always try to find a little something to be grateful for every morning. I'm just... well, I'm just missing my sister, you know? I haven't really noticed feeling deeply sad all the time like that. I hope I haven't made it sound like I'm falling apart or complaining too much today. It's perfectly normal to just feel a little off during a time like this without it being anything too serious, isn't it? I just want to be sure I'm handling things appropriately and not overreacting.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for more than two months?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, definitely not that long. I really wouldn't let myself just sit around and mope for two whole months, I promise. \n\nIt's only been... well, right around a month, actually. Just since my sister passed away. It was very unexpected, so it threw me for a bit of a loop, I suppose. But I'm really trying my best to get back on track and not let it keep me down. \n\nI mean, a month isn't too long to still be feeling a little out of sorts after a shock like that, is it? I just want to make sure I'm moving forward the way I'm supposed to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Well, I... I really do try to keep active, I promise I'm not just isolating myself. I still make myself show up for things, like my book club, and the community dance night. I know how important it is to keep socializing, especially at my age. 

But... oh, I don't know if this is the right way to describe it, but lately, when I'm there, I just sit there feeling like I'm behind a pane of glass. Like I'm watching it all happen but I'm not really part of it. I feel terrible admitting that, I really do. I don't want to seem ungrateful for my friends or my hobbies. It's just taking a bit more effort to find the joy in them right now. That's normal though, isn't it? Just a temporary thing while I'm adjusting? I'm sure I'll be back to my old self soon, I really am trying.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, heavens no, not most of the day. I really wouldn't let myself just sit and dwell on things all day long, I promise you that. 

I make sure to get up, get dressed, and keep up with my routine. Even when I feel a bit disconnected, like I mentioned with the book club or the garden, I'm still out there doing things. I hope it's alright that I'm feeling a little disconnected like this right now? Is it normal to lose that spark for a little while?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things that wouldn't have bothered you before your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to dwell on things, because I know it doesn't do any good, but... I do find myself thinking about those last few weeks with her. \n\nI keep going over it in my head, and I just... I keep telling myself I should have called her more often, or maybe listened a little better when we did talk. I just feel like I should have done something more useful for her, you know? \n\nI mean, part of me knows—logically, anyway—that I couldn't have changed what happened. I really do know that. But I still feel this awful weight about it. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself. It's just hard not to feel like I let her down somehow. Is it normal to feel this way after losing someone? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I don't believe so. I certainly hope I haven't come across as fidgety to you! I always try very hard to just sit quietly and, well, not make a fuss. \n\nI haven't really felt the need to pace around or anything like that. I just really wouldn't want to bother anyone by being restless or pacing about. I know some people get very agitated when they're going through a hard time, but I promise I've just been trying to stay calm. Is it okay that I haven't been feeling that way?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these thoughts of not wanting to wake up for most of the day, or are they more fleeting?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, not the whole day. I really try my best to shake it off once I'm out of bed, I promise. I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around dwelling on dark things all afternoon. \n\nIt's mostly just... well, it's in the mornings. I feel just awful even admitting this out loud, because I know I have a lot to be thankful for, but... I keep having these mornings where I just lie there and think it might be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. \n\nHonestly, having those thoughts is what scared me enough to finally come in and ask for help. I just want to be absolutely clear with you, though—I don't have any sort of plan to hurt myself or anything like that! I would never, ever do that. I know better than that. It's just this fleeting, heavy feeling when I first open my eyes. I hope it's alright that I'm sharing that with you? It's just a bit frightening to feel that way, and I want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by telling you.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I haven't really been keeping track on a scale or anything like that. I certainly haven't been trying to lose weight, I promise you that. I know it's important to keep my strength up. \n\nIt's just that... well, food just doesn't really call to me much anymore. I'll make a point to fix myself a little something, maybe some toast or a small bowl of soup, because I know I should eat. But then I'll look at the clock in the evening and realize I've barely eaten anything all day. \n\nI really do try to eat, though! I don't want to be a bother or end up making myself sick. I just don't have much of an appetite lately. I hope you don't think I'm neglecting myself on purpose. Do you think it's okay if I just eat small things for now, until my appetite comes back?\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two months of these feelings since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, it hasn't been that long. I wouldn't want you to think I've just been letting this drag on for months without trying to get a handle on it myself. I really do try to manage things. \n\nIt's only been about a month... ever since she passed away so unexpectedly. I tried my best to keep a brave face and just push through it, I really did. I didn't want to come running to a doctor right away if it was just normal grief, you know? I hope it was the right thing to wait a little while before coming in? I just wanted to be sure I wasn't wasting your time. But yes, it's just been this past month. I really hope I can get back to my normal self soon.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the things you used to enjoy have lost their appeal to you most of the time since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I still make myself go to my book club, and I still show up for the dance night at the community center, because I know people expect me there and I don't want to let anyone down. \n\nIt's just that... oh, I feel terrible saying this, but lately when I'm there, I just kind of sit there feeling like I'm behind a pane of glass. Like I'm watching everyone else enjoy themselves, but I just can't quite reach that feeling myself. But I just sit there feeling so detached, like I'm watching it all from behind a pane of glass. I just want to be sure I'm handling things appropriately and not overreacting.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for more than two months?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, definitely not that long.
